Imphal, the 29th August, 1955 . 
No. J/ 17 /55 . The following notification issued by the Central Government under 
Section 12 of the Commissions of Inquiry Act, 1952 is published for general infor 
mation : 

No. 16 / 1/ 65 - Judicial (I) 

Government of India 
Ministry of Home Affairs , 

NOTIFICATION 

New Delhi- 2 , the 26th July, 1966. 
In exercise of the powers conferred by section 12 of the Commissions of Inquiry 
Act, 1952 (LX of 1952), the Central Government hereby makes the following rules 
for the issue and service of summons by a Commission of Inquiry appointed by 
that Government , namely ; 

Rules 
1. The Commission may issue summons to persons whose attendanoe is 

required either to give evidence or to produce documents . 
2. Every summons issued by the Commission shall be in duplicate and 

signed by the Chairman or such person as be empowers in this behalf 
and sealed with the seal of the Commission , and it shall specify the 
time and place at which the person summoned is required to attend 
and also whether his attendance is required for the purpose of giving 

evidence or to produce a document, or for both purposes. 
3. A person may be summoned to produce a document, without being 

summoned to give evidence ; aud any person summopod merely to produce 
& document shall be deemed to have complied with the summons if 
he causes such document to be produced instead of attending , personally 

to produce the s & oje . 
4. A summons to produce documents may be for the produotion of certain 

specified documents or for the production of all documents of a certain 

description in the possession or power of the person summoned . 
6. Every summons shall be served by sending by post 

to the person 
for whom it is intended or in such other manner as the Commission 

may direct. 
5. The provisions of the foregoing roles shall apply , so far es may be, to 

every other proces issued bv the Commission : 


A. K. Bay 


-

No. G -DPC / 17 /55 .-- Whereas it appears to the Chief Commissioner that improved 
Irrangements are required within the area specified in the Soheduled hereunder, for 
Il matters as specified in Section 52 of the Assam Munioipal Act 1923 ( 1 of 1923 ) 
his extended to this State , which area it is not expedient to constitute into 
Municipality ; 

Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred upon him by sub - section 
( 1 , of Section 328 of the said Act, the Chief Commissioner desires to declare the 
Baid area as & notified area ; 

Should any ivhabitant of the said area desire to objeot to this notification , he 
may within six weeks from the date of publication of this notification in the State 
Gazette submit his objection in writing to the Chief Commissioner through the 
Deputy Commissioner for consideration . 

T. Kipgon, 
Sucretary to the Govt . of Manipur . 


Schedule 
(i) Commencing from the Indo - Burma and Thoubal Mayang - Imphal Road 

orosaing point , the boundary line runs towards the West along the boundary 
line of Dag No. 14 till it touches the northern boundary of the land covered 
by Dag No. 13 whence the line runs in straight and joins the point of 
north western corner of Dag No. 7. Then the same line TUDs right 
up, following the hundary line of Dag . No. 7 in circumference , the 
line then meets the north eastern corner of the Dag referred to from 

where it originated , 
( ii ) Commencing from the crossing point where the Indo -Burma Road and 

Thoubal River meet with each other, the line runs on the southern 
embankment of the same river, till it reaches the north west corner 
of Dag No. 1347. Then it follows the north eastern boundary of the 
same Dag in the straight line till it meets the old Yairipok Road under 
Dag No. 1031 and then it runs southward along the eastern boundary 
of Dag No. 1033,1034 , 1036 and 1037 , then it goes along the north 
eastern boundary of Dags 1038 and 1041 and southern boundary of 
Dag No. 1041 till it meets Burma Road . Theoce it follows the eastern 
boundary of Burma Road and meets the crossing point where it started

২। ২ লেজগনি । লিলাম সুমিতা শেল শুনা খিৰিগলবনি ।

The following reso utions are passed : 
1 . The Manipur Drivers Union , who are the only applicants for a permit to ply 

a passenger cum goods motor vehicular service between Churachandpur and 
Thingat are given the monopoly to do so for one year w o. f. 1.9.55 subject 
to such conditions as may be iroposed from time to time by the Manipur 
State Transport Authority regarding periodicity of service , rates of freight 

and fares and pay - load etc. 
2 . The question of fixation of the number of Stage carriages and Public Car 

riers which may be required exclusively for each and every particular route 
in the State as well as for the Dimapur-Manipur Road and determining road 
permit fees separately for these routes is considered to be of mnoh impor 
tance requiring deeper consideration and hence should be takon up in the 
next meeting of the S. T. Authority . In the meantime , the Secretary will 
please collect all relevant data and circulate the same with his suggestions 
to all the members ,

AS -AM PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSION 
No. 3064/55 . PSC. - It is hereby notified that the next Half - Yearly Departmental 
Examination will be held on Monday , the 21st November, 1955 and the following 
three days at Silchai and Gauhati. In case of Non -Gazetted Police Officers the 
examination will be held in the Headquarters of the district in which they may 
be serving at the time with the exception of the Police Officers serving in the 
Naga Hills District and the Non -Gazetted Police Officers of Manipur State who will 
have to appear at Jorbat. Candidates who intend to appear at this examination 
should communicate their names through the proper channel in accordance with the 
orders communicated in Government Memo. No. AAP. 83/51/63, dated the 25th Angust , 
1951 so as to reach this office on or before the 20th October 1955 possitively . Under 
no circumstances officers who in due time and through proper channel do not intimate 
their intention to appear at the examination will be allowed to sit al the examination . 
Officers should fill their names in full (no initials ) and in blook letters in the letter 
of intimation . The programme of the examination is as follows :

No. 7.7.Pross/ pub /55. - The following Notice is published for general information . 

Gopendrt Sharma, 

Publicity Officer , Manipur 
Imphal , the 26th September, 1955 . 
With effect from the 28th September , 1955, a daily courier service from Imphal 
o Tamenglong and vice verse will be set up to curry mails between these two head . 
quarters . Lamboos of the Deputy Commissioner s establishment will work as couriers. 
The courier service will be open to utilisation by all Government Departments in 
mphal as well as in Tumenglong . All mails intended for delivery to the 8. D. O., 
ameuglong or other Officers posted there should be handed over to the Deputy Commis 
ioner s Office and those to be despatched from Tamenglong to Imphal by Officers 
bere should be delivered to the Office of the S.D.O., Tamenglong for onward trans 
viesiou to the respective destinations. Mails should be handed over to the offices 
jentioned during office hours on working days and at 17 A.M. on holidays, 

Denartments concerned will please direct their messengers to the respective offices 
t Imphal and at Tamonglong overy day to collect their mails . 
This arrangement will continue until further orders. 

V. S. HUNDARAM , 

Deputy Commissioner, Manipur .

MINISTRY OF INFORMATION AND BROADCASTIxG 

RESOLUTION 

New Delhi, the 2nd February 1955 . 
No. 1 /51/54.F .-- Whereas the Film Advisory Board has been functioning for recom 
mending documentaries and newsrdels produced by the Films Division and by private 
producers for grant of approval certificates with reference to the televant condition in 
the cinema licences and whereas a direction has now been issued by the Central Govern 
mient under bilb -section (4 ) of section 12 of the Cinematograph Act, 1952 (XXXVII of 
1952), and by the various State Governments under their own Acts , it has been decided 
that the Film Advisory Board should be entrusted with the responsibility of approving 
films which may be regarded as scientific films, films intended for educational purposes, 
films dealing with news and current events or documentary films with reference to these 
directions . 

2. The rules governing the working of the Film Advisory Board will be as follows : 
(1) The Board shall consist of not less than seven members , to be appointed by 
the CentralGovernment , as follows : 

( a) Chairman of the Central Board of Film Censors — Ex-Officio Chairman. 
(b ) Regional Officer of the Central Board of Film Censors , Bombay - Exc 

Officio member . 
( c) A member of the Advisory Patel of the Central Board of Film Cenšors 

at Bombay 
(d ) Not less than four other members including an educational and two 

persons with knowledge of films and film making . 


MANIPUR GAZETTE , OCTOBER , 1956. 


Explanation . - " Regional Officer" inolades " Additional Regional Officer" and " Assistant 
Regional Officer" . 


(2) Members other than the ex -officio members and the member of the Advisory 
Panel of the Central Board of Film Censors, Bombay, shall be appointed for u period for 
two years and will be eligible for re -appointment. The member of the Advisory Panel 
sball be appointed as a member of the Board for the full term of his membership of the 
Panel. 


(3 ) The Guvernment of India may for a cause sufficient in its opinion , rem ve any 
member of the Board before the expiration of the period of his office. 

(4) Tbe nod-official members of the Board will work in an honorary capacity. They 
may be paid such compensatory allowance as the Central Goveroment may from time 
to time determine . 


(6 ) The Regional Officer of the Central Board of Film Censors at Bombay will be 
the Secretary of the Board . 


( 6 ) No act or proceeding of the Board shall be called in question on the ground 
merely of the existence of any Vacancy in or defect in the constitution of the Board . 


( 7) Unless otherwise directed by the Government of India , the Headquarters of the 
Board shall be at Bombay . 


(8 ) Meetings of the Board shall be presided over by the Chairman , and , in his 
absence the members present may elect one among themselves to preside. 


( 9) The quorum of the Board shall be three . 


(10 ) The decision of the Board shall be that of the majority of the members attend 
ing the meeting and in the event of an equality of votes, the presiding officer shall 
have a second or casting vote . The decision of the Board shall be final. If any appli 
cant wants to make any representation in regard to the film submitted by bin , however , 
the Board shall give him an opportunity to do so and it shall beopen to the Bnard 
to revise their decision in the light of any such representation . 

( 11 ) The Board shall be ordinarily meet every week for the purpose of approving 
films as scientific film , films intended for educational purposes , films dealing with 
news and current events or documentary films in terms of the directions issued by the 
Government of India under sub -section (4 ) of section 12 of the Cinematograph Act, 1952 
(XXXVII of 1954) and any similar directions issued by the State Governments. 

(12 ) Any person , firm , organisation or Government desirous of having any of his 
or its films approved by the Board shall send an application in writing to the Secretary 
of the Board stating the title of the film and the s jurcs from which it can be obtained 
by the exbibitors and such other particulars as may be required by the Board . 

( 18 ) The Board will opmmuntoate the titles sul subjezte of the films approved by 
thena , together with the information regarding the sources from which tbey can be 
obtained , to the Central Government. The titles of the film , certified to be scientific 
films, films intended for educational purposes , films dealing with news and current 
events or docunentary films by the Central Government , with the approval of the filma 
Advisory Board , will be notified in the Gazette of India together with the address from 
which they can be obtained . 

P. M.LAD , Secy .

PART II 


NOTIFICATION . 


Dated Shillong, the 5th September , 1955 . 


: 


Subject :-Verification by the Treasury Officer of countersignature on the Travel 

ling allowance claim of Gazetted Government servant for the period 

prior to his traasfer to the payment of a new Treasury Officer. 
No. TM./4/55/26.-A reference is invited to rule 177 

of the Central Treasury 
Rule Vol-I , which inter -alia imposes an obligation on the Treasury Officer to verify 
the genuineness of the countersignature of the Controlling Officer on the Travel 
ling allowance claims of a gazetted Officer s bill. A question has been raised as to 
how a Treasury Officer is to satisfy himself of the genuineness of the counter 
sigoature of the Controlling Officer in respect of a claim prior to the transfer of 
the officer to his payment. As the matter stands at present, no provision exists in 
the Treasury Rules providing for the action to be taken by the now Treasury 
Officer in this regard . To meet this lacuna in the rules, it has been decided that 
all the controlling Officers empowered to countersign bills should send their speci 
men signatures to all the Treasury Officers duly attested by the Treasury Officer 
of the district, in which they draw bills so 

that the new Treasury Officer inay 
not have any difficulty to verify the countersignaturo. 

The Controlling · Officers (Central ) and all the Treasury and Sub- Treasury Officers 
in Assam , Tripura and Manipur will please see that the procedure detailed above 
in this regard is strictly followed . 


Amitabha Ghosh , 
Deputy Accountant General (II), Aegam .

been considered and it has been decided that qucta licences inued to Establisher

condition mentioned in rewark (ii ) against S. No. 122( vii )/V in Section II of this 
ourrent Red Book. It will thus be open to Established importers with quot

ANNEXURE 
SCHEME FOR MUTUALISATION UNDER SECTION 60 OF THE 

INSURAVCE ACT, 1938 . 
1. In this scheme, the following words and phrases shall have the meanings 
respectively as hereinafter mentioned 

" The Act " shall mean the Insurance Act , 1938 . 

" Appointed Date " shall mean such date, after the date of sanction of this Schome 
by the Central Government , as shall be notified in that bebalf by the Original 
Company . 

" Original Com ; a ov ” shall mean the Oriental Government Security Life Assurance 
Jempany Limited us at present constituted . 

“ Mutual Company ” shall mean the Oriental Government Security Life Assurance 
Company Limited is re - constituted under the provisions of this Scheme as . Mutual 
Company 

" Policy holder " shail ii clude , in the case of policies of assurance , the proposer or 
each of the proposers thereunder, and in the case of annuity contracts , the 
purchaser or oich ot the purchasers thereof Provided ( a ) that a policyholder who 
has assigned or transferred the whole of his interost in the policy to any person 
or persons other than a trustee or trustees, or his employers, in accordance with the 
rules of any Provident Fund Scheme, or any Staff Insurance or other Scheme inten 
ded for the benefit of employees shall not be deemed to be a policy holder , and ( b ) 
that a policy shall be deemed to be subsistıng si long as it is in force according 
to the conditions thereof, and has not lapsed without securing a paid -up value or 
been cancelled , and the sum assured under the policy has not become payable or 
in the case of an annuity contract, the annuity has not ceased . 

2 . The Oriental Government Security Life Assurance Company Limited sball , as 
from the Appointel Date , c ase to be a company having a share capital, an l shall 
be re -constituted under the prorisio is of section BC of the sct as 

& company limited 
by guarantee, and not having a share capital. 

* 3 . The Memorandum and Articles of Association andexed hrreto shall be the 
Memorandum and Articles of Association of the Company as re -constituted . 
4. As from the Appointed Date , the following consequences shall engue : 
(a ) ali the propertima, esets and moneya of the Original Company represen 

ting its Share Capital and all its Funds of various kinds shall be taken 
over by the Miitual Company and all the limbilities under policiis and 
all other liabilities of the Original Company shall be taken over by the 

Mutual Cumpany. 
( b ) The Directors of ihe Original Company shall continue to function as 

Directors of this vutial Company until the first Annual General Meeting 
of the futual Company when the first Directors of the Mutual Company , 
except those who have been elected under the provisions of Section 48

It is bereby notified for general information that the annual auction sale of grass 
mabals will take place in the Forest Office, Imphal at 11-0 A. M. daily , commencing 
from 12th October, 1955 . 

The list of grass mabals to be sold on each day is given below with dates . 

The successful bidders shall have to pay the sale proceeds of the grass mahuls in 
full on the day of the sile . In case of inpability to pay the sale price on the 
day of sale , the grass mahal will be resold at the risk of former bidder. 

With a view to know the existing grass contents of the grass mahals , the inten 
ding contractors or 

may visit and inspeot the grass mahabs for which 
they wunt to bid in the auction . When the auction is con lucted and the price 
of a particular grass mabal is fixed , no future complaint for reduction in the sale 
price , on grounds of paucity of grass contents or any other cause , will be considered . 

Any one who wishes to lodge a reasonable complaint, on a partioular grass mahal, 
he should apply to the Forest Department some days ourlier that the auction takes 
place.

EARLY PAYMEYT OF COMPENSATION TO WIDOWS. 
In a Press Note dite ] July 29 , 1963 it was announced that widows baving verified 
claims in their own names otc . would be paid cash upto a maximum of Rs. 8,000 / - and 
would be given priority in the matter of payment. 

This concession is uw being extended to those who became widows subsequent to the 
filing of compensation is plications by their husbands. 

Such widows, whether or not their applications were mydo in their own names, will 
be given priority in the matter of the payment of compensation . 


EXPLOITATION OF SMALL DISPLACED CLAIMANTS . 
GOVERNMENT NOT TO RECOGNISE SALE AND PURCHASE OF CLAIMS. 

PAYMENT OF COMPENSATION TO BE EXTENDED . 
The Government of India Hirve notioed with some concern that the concession which 
was given to displaced persons with claims who wished to purchase property in the 
Com ensation pool, 10 associate other claimants with them to make up the amount of their 
bid , is being abused . 

Some persons interested in the purchase of pool property have been including those 
claimants who are in need of ready cash or part with their claims at prices far below the 
amount of compensation payable on such claims. The oliject of the Government in por 
mitting claimants to associates among themselves for the purpose of buying property was 
to facilitate both the purchise and the sale of such property and to enable genuine buyers 
to purchase property itaingt daims. 

To put an end to exploitation , thom Gorornmert has decided not to recognise sale and 
purchase of claims. 

Such sale ar d purchase of claims will not be recognised . However, if claimants wish 
to combine to purchase property , they would be permitted to do so , but il sale certificate 
will be made out in the name of all these persons and their shares specified in it according 
to the amount contributed by each of them either by adjustment of claim or by cash . 

To give immcdiate relief to small claimants . orders have already issued that those 
claimants whose claims are for Rs. 2,000 or legs should be given priority for payment in 
cash . 

Cash payments upto Rs . 8,000 will continue to be made to claimants holonging to 
needy categories, such as widows, grantees of maintenance allowance and displaced 
claimants who are suffering from T.B. or cancer or whose dependants are suffering from 
these diseases, minors, inmates of Homes, receipients of cash doles, etc. 

The range of persons to whom early relief should he given by payment of compen 
fation in cash is being further extended . 

Instructions have been issued that claimants whose verified claims do not exceed 
Pos . 10,000 and who are to be paid in cash should have their cases dealth with 
expeditiotisly .

নােলিশ । 

নং STO/ 11 / 58ং ১৩১২৫৫ । 
এ পুমা খঞ্জরি মাহদি মণিপুর গভর্ণমেন্টক প্রেস নােট নং তাং ২০১০৫৫ ইংগী মতুং ইন্না চংলি 
ডিসেম্বর বাণী তাং ১৫ দগী হোন। ষ্টেট ট্রেডিং অফিসনা পয়লা নম্বৰগী চেং লৌবাশিংগী ইঙ্কালগা লৈ হেগনি । 
চেী মমলদি বােড়া হাওনা মনদা সুপ ৯ (মাপল) নি । হাৱা গড়া , গাড়ীদগী থাংথবা, ওনবা অমসুং গুণাদা - 
নিবগী (ষ্টোক তৌবগী) খরচ চেং তীবা মীনা পগদবনি । বোড়াদি অহনা ওইগনি অমসুং বােড়। অন্য যে 
মন অনিমখাই হাপরুদবনি । চেং তন্মৰিবা মা খুদিংমকা “ লৌউবা মীনি অমসুং তস্মরি চেং অদু মশা মশানা বা 
লৌগ থােকপা চেনি হায়না অইবা চে অমা (ডিক্লারেশন অমা ) খুনগী থাবা বা স্বাক্ষী অনি ও সহী 
তো পৰিগনি। 

ষ্টেট ট্রেডিং অফিসন। লেগদবা চেং নমুনা (সেম্পল ) আফিস্যা থমজরে । চেং তীগে হায় লৌবা মী মখৈন 
অফি । লাকুনা সেম্পল যেংবিবাদৰনি । কনাগুম্বা মীনা সেম্পদগী তানা চেং পুবিরকলবদি চেং লৌজরােই (রিজেক্ট 
তৌজগনি) । 

চে লৌৰ মৰেদি কৈধেলগ ওইনা মাদ । পীজরি তারিখ অমসুং মনশিংগী মতুংইয়া বাজার বাজান কোরিয়া 
খুলশিংদা লৈ দৌৰাশিংগী অমুক পুং ১ • দগী নুংখিল পু ! অনি ফাও লেজগনি । নােংমদ । পৰি পৰিা বারণি 
মসশিং অদুদগী হেলা পুবিরকপা অদুগ পায়ী অফিসনা পুজরােই, অহেবা চেং মখৈ লেবোই । সীগদ 
শিংনা ইট ট্রেভি আফিসকী কোম্বাে বামদা অকে ? অহান নাইন। লেংদুনা লৈৰিগৰনি । 
ম অমুক লোমদাইদ অপ্পো নােটিশ গম। অমুক তে বলি ।

নােটিশ নং ২৩- ১১১৫৫ ইং 
অসিনা মরুষ ওইদুনা প্রজা পুমা খংহঞ্জরি । মরমদি মনিপুরগী এলাকা মনুদা ভেলি মহল, নােপােৰু থংৰ । 
ভিতিন, গম জিরি বােরাক ফোরে , নোংচুপ থবা ডিভিজন, অসিদগী খােইক , শামু ময়া , শজি মচিনচিংবা থিৰা 
অং সল্লোনগী মােনােপােলি অসি ইংশােক ১৯৫৫ জুন খাগী তাং ১ দগী হোন। ইং ১৯৫৬ গী মে থাগী তাং ৩১ 
ফাওবা , মিঃ চিকো এণ্ড সন্স মেক্স -মেল বাজার ইস্টালদা লেবা অসিদা মােনতে । 

কনাগুনা হাবিবা পােংশি অসি লৈগে অমসুং মােনগে হায়বদি মথ । হায়ঞ্জিরা পাটাভার অসিদা চংপিগণৰনি । 
পাটাতার অসি নতুবা অতােপপ কনাগুম্বন। পােৎশিং অসি লৈব। যেনিবা তৌরমলবদি মতিক চাকা দণ্ডি তৌগনি । 
পােৎশিং অসি বৈ ( থ ) মনিপুর টেকী মংদা লৈব। চীং অমসুং মগ ম 

অমসুং মপ মী পুমা পােংশিং অসি খুদক 
অনুমা মথী মােনােপােলি অসিদা য়োনন পাবীগদৰনি নবদি পােংশিং অসি বনমলিসা মী অমদি মীশিং অদ্র 
কি চাষ । দণ্ডি তৌগনি অমসুং পােৎশিং অদুস্থ মৎকনি ।

ফোরেষ্ট অফিসার মনির । 
নোটিশ নং ১৩/৫৫ — তাং ১৪।১২৫৫ ইং 
নােটিশ অসিনা মরম ওইদুনা প্রজা পুমা খংহঞ্জরি । শনপােৎ খােইষদ শিজিল্পনা মশাফৰা নহা ওইয়িবা শল 
পাৰা অনিখক লৌজগদৌরি । পিবিনিংবা মীনা চাক্ষুষ নম্বা টেণ্ডর তাং ২৩১২৫৫ ইংগী মনুদা এগ্রিকলচয়েল অফি 
থাবিরকপা মাগনি । তাং ২৪১২৫৫ ইংগী পুং ১ তাৰ মতমদা টেণ্ডর হাংগনি । টেণ্ডর খাৰিকপা মী খুদিংমা মুমি 

হুমা শলশিং অদু পুদবনি । টেণ্ডর অফাওবা মীনা সুপা ০৩ সিকিওরিটি থগিদবনি অমসুং ষ্টেগুর ফাণ্ডৰ 
তুমিৎ ১৫নিগী মনুংদা শলশিং অনু উখল ফার্ম অফিতা পিগদৰনি । পুথােক -পুশিনগী খৰচ পুৱমক মাই ওইজগন্সবনি । 

শ্বাইদগী অপিকপা অমদি অতৈ টেণ্ডর লৌদবা আপত্তী লৈবােই । অকুপা মরােল একিলচরেল অফিস 
শিব মিগনি । 

L. Udhob Singh , 
Agricultural Officer, Manipuri

W bereas a book entitled “ Ajod Chitra printed by Shri V. G. Mebendale at 
. rabodh Mudranalaya , 438-4 , Sada biv Peth , Poona 2 , contains objectionable matter 
o the ground that pictures therein particularly the 9th , 35th , : 2nd and both pictures 
in the said book are obsvene, within the meaning of clau - 6 ( vi) of section 3 of 
he Press (Ohjectionable Matter) Act, 1951 (LVI of 1951) ; 

And whereas the Advocate General, Bomhay , has granted a certificato under 
poction 11 of the said Act that the said pictures are objectionable matter within 
be meaning of clause (vi) of section 3 of the said Act ;

powers

“ Show house" means any building , tent, or any roofed and 
onclosed structure , used ordinarily or occasionally for the demousta 
tion or exhibition to the publio , whether on payment or otherwis , of 
cinematographic films, drunjatical or musical performances , dances, 
physical feats of human beings or animals , conjuring tricks or sleights 
of hand , or any other indoor amusement whatsover, but does not 
inoludu bucla building or tent or roofed or enclosed structure in 

which meals are served along with the demonstration or exhibition 
3. Whoever smokes , during a demon - tration , or exhibition , in any 

part of a show house reserved for the audience or the spectators 
shall be punishable with fine which may extend to one hundred 
rupees. 
Explanation - For the purpose of this section " demonstration 
exhibition ” gliall be deemed to commence when tbe audience or the 
spectators or any part of them have entered the show housy to 
witness & show therein und tu continue until they have left the 

house after the close of the show . 
4. Any police officer not below the rank of Sub- Inspector may arrest 

without warrant any person committing an offence under section % 

in his presence . 
5 . ()) Every person responsible for the management of a demonstra 

tion or exhibition in a show house shall brug to the norice of the 
audience or spectators , by posting notices pruininantly or by exhi 
biting slider , that any per-on smoking dur ng a demonstration or 
eshibition , in any part of such house reserved for the audience or 
sprclators shall be liable to arrest without warrint and to fine. 
( 2 ) Whoever contravents the privisions of sub -section (1 ) shall be 

punishable with fine which may extend to two hundred rupees . 
6. The ( "hief Commissioner or any otficer of the State Government 

authorised in this behilf by general or special order in writing 
direct that the provisions of this Act shall not apply in respect 
of any show house or any demonstration or esihibition therein .

ভারতকী মরু ওইনা কমুনিটি প্রােজেক্ট অমসুং ন্যসমেল এক্সটেনসন সাভিশ যােক এরিয়াদপী শিউবা 
থৱাই মাওবা নহারােল মুপা ২৫ অমসু প ১০ রোম আমেরিকাগ লে উশিংউপ মওং মতো অমসুং শজৎনচিংবা 
তমলুন International Farm youth Exchange programme গ মতুং ইন্না মথং চহিপ এপ্রিল অমসুং 
মে থাপা আমেরিকা । থাগদে রি । 


চাওরাকনা চহি ১৯ দগী মনুদা লৈবা হৌক্তিকমক কলেজ তমদ্ৰবা অমসুং ইংরেজি নিংথিনা বা উৰা মীশিংনা 
দরখাস্থ থবিরকপা য়াগনি । চংকদৌরিবা মীশিংনা আমেরিকাগ লৌউবশিংগী অতিথি ওইনা লৈরুগদবনি অদুগা যুধু 
অদুগা লেবুক্তা থবক শুমিদুনা থা ৫৬ লােম লৈগদবনি । আমেরিকা যৌনবা অমসুং মফম অদুদগী হল্পকনবা ভাড়া 
সুপা ২,৫০০ অমসুং মরন্নাই খরচ ওইনবা লুপা ১,২০০ রোম পুথোকচগদবনি । লৈফম চাফমগী খরচাদি তিংলােই । 
আমেরিকা বিশ্ববিদ্যালয়দা লাইরিক তম্বা যারােই নগা ভারা হল্লকলগা থবক বিবা যারােই । ভায়া হল্পকলগা 
লৌউশিউবা নহারােলশিংগী শেমগং শাগংপণ্ঠ বক্তা মতেং পাংবিগদবনি । 


দরখাস্বকী ফোম অমসুং অতৈ অকুপ্পা মবােল এগ্রিকালচর আফিসগ খংবিবা আগনি ।

the following section shall be inserted , rewely . 

" 479A Procedure in certain cases o false evidence - 1) Notwithstan ling anythin ; con 
tained in sections 478 to 479 inclusive, when any Civil, Revenue or Criminal Court is 
of opinion that any person appearing before it as a witness 

a wituess las intentionally given false 
evidence in any stage of the judicial proceeding or has intentionally fabricated false evidence 
for the purposo of being used in any stage of the judicial proceeding , and that, for the 
eradication of the evils of perjury and fabrication of false eviden :e and in the interests of 
justice , it is expedient that such witness should be prosecuted for the offence which appears 
to have been committed by him , the Court shall, at the time of the dolivery of the judg . 
ment or final order disposing of such proceeding , record a finding to that effeot atating its 
reagong therefor and may , if it so thinks fit , after giving the witness an opportunity of 
boing heard , make a complaint thereof in writing signed by the presiding officer of the 
Court setting forth the evidence which , in the opinion of the Court, is false or fabricated 
and forward the same to a Magistrale of the first class having jurisdiction , anl may, if 
the accused is present before the Court, take sufficient security for his appearance before 
such Magistrate and may bind over any persun to appear you give evidence befora snob

90. INSERTION OF NEW SECTION 485A , IN ACT V or 1898. - After section 485 of the principal 
Act, the following section shall be inserted namely : 

" 485A . Summary procedure for punishment jor non - attendance by a witness in obedience to 
summons.- (1) If any witness being summoned to appear before a Criminal Court is legally 
bound to appear at a certain plaro and time in obedience to the summons and without jast 
dicuse neglects or refuses to attend at that place or time or departs from tho place where 
ho has to attend before the time at which it is lawful for hiin to depart , and the Court 
before which the witness is to appear is satisfied that it is expedient in the interests of 
justice that such witness should be tried summarily , the Court may take cognizance of the 
offence and after giving the offender an opportunity of showing cause why he should not 
be punished under this section , sentence him to fine not exceeding one hundred 

rupees . 
( 2 ) In every such case the Court shall follow , as nearly as may be practicable , the 
procedure prescribed for summary trails in which an appeal lies."

lated September 10, 1951, under rule 96 of the Displace i Persuns (Conipensation and

zrants from all displacet persons having n ) veritied claims who have left immovable

Applications for rehabilitation grant should be made on the prescribed form and 
handed over or sent to the Settlement Officer within whose jurisdiction the appli 
cant resides , or carries on business , or works for gain . The prescribed forms are 
on sale at one anna per form at the office of the Chief Settlement Commissioner , 
Metcalfe House , Delhi, offices of all the Regional Settlernent Commissioners und all Settle 
ment Offices under the Central Ministry of Rehabilitation . Arrangements are also being 
made to make the forms available at post offices . 
Every application for rehabilitation grant shall be accompanied : 
(i) by a fee of eight annas only in the form of a postal order for this 
amount, crossed in the 

name of the Settlement Officer concerned , 
provided that where the person liable to pay the fue resides vutside India , 
à receipt of the amount of the fee liyan officer authorised in this 
behalf by the representative of India in the cuntry where such person 

resides , shall be sufficient evidence about the payment of the fee ; 
(ii) in the case of a displaced person arriving in India after the introduc 

tion of the passport system ( October 15 , 1952) by a truo copy of the 

migration certificate ; 
(iii) in the case of any other displaced person, by other satisfactory proof 

of arrival in India after July 31, 1952 ; and 
(iv ) by an affidavit containing particulars of any property left in West Pakistan . 
Applicants should fill in all the parti ulars required in the form Crefully, 
as to avoid delay which will be caused if inaccurate or incomplete information is given . 
The last date for the filing of applications is December 9 , 1956 . 

Displaced Persons entitled to file applications for rehabilitation grants under this 
notification , who are residing outside In lia , should apply to the Chief Settlement 
Commissiuner , Metcalfe House, Civil Lines , Delhi, either directly or through a duly 
appointed agent, or through the accredited Missions abroad . 
Ministry of Rehahilitation . 
New Delhi, September 10, 1956 .

Gopendra Sharma , 
l ublicity Officer , Manipur, 


PRESS INFORMATION BUREAU : GOVERNMENT OF INDIA . 

PRESS NOTE . 
DELIVERY OF BOOKS TO PUBLIC LIBRARIES 
The Government of India have declared the Central Library , Town Hall , Bombay , 
as one of the public libraries under the Delivery of Books (Public Librarice) Act, 1954 . 

The Act provides for the free and compulsory supply of one copy of all books 
published in India to the National Library, Calcutta und to each of three public 
libraries so declared hy the Government of India . 

Sometine ago the Connermura Public Library, Madras was declared as a " Public 
Library ." The present notification brings the number of " Public Libraries " to two, 
excluding the National Library , Calcutta . The third " Public Library " has yet to be 
specified . 

The Central Government desire to draw the attention of all publisbers of books 
in India that under the paid Act , failure to supply the books to the Nationa ! Li 
brary , Calcutta, Connermara Public Library , Madras, and the Central Library, Town 
Hell , Bombay , will be an offence under the Act . 

The object of the Act was to secure for the country four libraries in four diffe 
ront parts of the country, wbich , by stocking all books published in India , will acte 
as Centres for the promotion of kuowledge and research . 
Ministry of Education 
Now Doth , November, 7, 1967.

Subject : -- Centenary of the Indian Patont system . 
I am directed to say that one hundred 

years will have passed on the 28th 
Frebuary , 956 , since the first Indian Patents Act ( Ict VI of 185 ) for the protec 
tion an : encouragement of inventions cime into force . The Govt. of India have 
decided to celebrate the contenary of the Patent system in India in February next 
year. The celebration will be held in Calcutta and will commence on the 28th 
February , 1956 . 

2 . The Ranji Stadium at Calcutta has been tentatively selected as & probable 
site for the exhibition , The programme will include an exhibition of patented ma 
chines and articles , registered designs and also products of Indian manufacture , a 
symposium on patents by industrialists , research scholars , lawyers and patent agents , 
and the publication of a Souvenir. The Ministry of Commerce and ludustry bave 
appointed a sub -committee composed of their officers located in Calcutta and repre 
sentatives of other Ministries to work out the details of the celebrations , 

3. Applications from private firms and individuals for space in the exhibition 
site , which will be rented out at Rs. 2 /- per sq . ft . should reach the Controller of 
Patents and Designs, 214 , Lower Circular Road , Calcutta , 17 , by the 30th Novem 
mber, 1955. Arrangements have been made tu grant import faciliti8 to exhibitors . 
4 . It is proposed to publish in the Sourvenir articles on Indian and 

and foreiga 
patent law and practice and other matters of iute est to industrialists and inventors. 
Advertisements may be inserted in the Souvenir at ratos approved by the Committee . 
which are Rs. 100 / - for a full pige , Rs. 60 , - for a half page and Rs . 30 / - for a 
quarter page 

5. A brochure containing informution and rules for the guidance of exhibitions 
is being issued shortly by the Controller of Petents and Designs. Calcutta , 

6. The Govern inent of India will be grateful if you will give the centenary 
celebrations the widest possible publicity in your State and also intimate to 
the Controller of Patents and Designs , the number of copies of the brochure 
required by you for distribution . Persons desirous of participating in the exhibition 
or inserting advertisements in the Souvenir or contributing articles for inclusion 
in the Souvenir , may be abvisel to communicato immodiately to the Controller of 
Patents and Designs, 214 , Lower Circular Road , Calceutta - 17 .

Imphal, the 20th October , 1955 . 
CENTRAL OVER -SEAS SCHOLARSHIP SCHEME 

1956-57 . 
No. V.7.PRESS/PUB/55.--The Government of India have decided to continue the 
Modified Overseas Scholarships Scheme, now known as the Central Oversgas/ Scholar 
ships Schemo, during the year 1956-87 . The Scheme is meant for Universities and 
comparable Institutions in order to enablo them to obtain apportunities for their teachers 
for higher studies / Traiving abroad and thus to raise the stundard of instruction and 
research , 


moro 


As to the financial implications the Government of India will contribute 50 per 
cent of the entire cost and the remaining 50 per cent will have to be borne by the 
sponsoring authorities. If University /State Government/ Institution decides to partici 
pate in the scheme, applications not than four of their nominees in the 
prescribed form along with application form for admission to an educational insti 
tution in the United Kingdom / The Continent/ Japan of the United States of America / 
Canada may be forwarded 80 as to reach the Ministry of Eucation , Government 
of India not later than the 15th November , 1955 . 

Forms and other information may be cullocted from the office of the Asstt. 
Secretary ( Education ) to the Government of Manipur . 


Gopendra Sarma, 
Publicity Officer, Manipur.

No. K / IN -3 /55.- The following Presy Note received from the Office of Textile Commis 
sioner , Bombay is republished for general in ormation . 

II . R. Singh , 
Asstt . S’cretary (GIL ) to the Govt. of Manipur. 


PRESS NOTE . 


Certain difficulties have been experience liy tbe Silk Trade in India regarding 
the clearanoe of their consignments of Tassar Silk Fabrios, exported to foreign countries, 
at the destination in the absence of a Certificate about the origin and contents of 
goods exported . In order to oviate these difficulties, it has been decided to introduce, 
with immediate effect , a procedure of certification by the Govt . of India . The procedure 
will be as follows. The exporters of silk Fuhrics in General and Taugar Silk Fabrics 
in particular will, in their own interest , forward to the Seci etary , Central Silk Board , 
office of the Textile Commissioner , Haraw.la Building , Wittet Road , Ballard Estate , 
Bombay- l , the samples of silk goods that they desile to export to foreign countries 
with nocessary details . The samples will be examined by the Board and itge sea ! 
affixed on the certifying the origin and contents of scoh samples and then returned 
to the Parties copoerood to effeot exports .

“ আপিল ” 

Imphal, the 8th November , 1956 . 
শাে ১১৫৫ গী ডিসেম্বর তাং ৭ অসি ভারত পুম্বগী গ্লগ ডে পালন্ তৌবা সুমিনি । মনিপুরসু সুমি অসি 
পাল ভৌগনি । মুমিং অসিদা ভারতকী মফন পুমা মচু অহুমগী ফারাল মচা অমসুং অচেৰা মখাদা উৎলিবা 
মম অসিদা য়ােনগনি । 

ফীরাল মচা ১ গ মল অনা 

অচৌবা , সুপা 
ফীরালশিং অসি নােবগী ফংলপ। শেল অনুন। ভারতকী সানমিশিং অমসুং লানমিক্ষপ থবক তােকল অশােন 
অনা সিফায়শিংগী কানবা থবক্তা শিজিন ফ্লেগ ডে ফন্ ওইগনি । 

মনিপুর্গ মফম পুমনমকসু হায়খ্রিবা তুমিৎ অসিদা ফীলশিং অসি যেনগনি । ফীরালশিংসি মােনদা প্রজাশিনা 
মতে পিবিরগা ফন্ অসি মপুং ফাকা গুমগনি । 

পুকচেন্ চাওবা দেশ বাসিশিংনা দানপমশ ওইনা ফীল অমমম লৈবিয়ু অমসুং অঙম অন্য মজে বিনা 
ফ্লেগ ডে ফন্ অসি নপুং ফাহনবিয়ু হায়না আপিল তোরি ।

ডি , এস, এণ্ড চেয়ায়ামে , 
গে ডে ফন্ কমিটি , মনিপুর ।

তোৰবমলা লৌউবদমক লিজ হােলদব ওইবা মী ১০২ লে পরি ৭৬ মরক অসি তাং ১১-১১-৫৫ ইংল্য কেক 
এতে দুন পােলিশকী জিমাদা থমলে হাবিবা পৌফম অসিদা হোবিব। ফে না অহ তা ২৫-১১-৫৫ ইং ইরাই মুমিক। 
অয়ুৰু পুং ১১ তাৰ মতমদা ইম্ফাল ওয়েষ্ট তহশীল আসিত। লৌফম যাওদনা নিলাম তৌছুল য়োনগনি ।

(In the appendix E to Vol. II of C.T.R., add the following as part III : ---- ). 

No. 96 . 
HINTS ON DETECTING COUNTERFEITS OF PURE NICKEL COINS. 


? 


reverse 


With the introduction of Pure Nickel series of coins in 1946, comprising Rupees , 1/2 
Rupees and 1/4 Rupees, the design of these coins was ohanged ; the sizes and standard 
weights of the coios remaining the same as in previous issues, except in the case of the 
Rupee, which became smaller in diameter and consequently thicker, the weight 18 
maining the same at 180 grains troy– the 1/2 Rupees and ! / 4 Rupees weighting 90 grains 
and 45 grains respectively . Pure Nickel rupees were first issued bearing the date 1947 
and 1/2 Rupees and 1/4 Rupees bearing the date 1946 Puse Nickel riparu of 1947 
and 1/2 rupees aad 1/4 rupees of 1946 aud 1947 bors on their Reverse the Tiger and on 
the Obverse the effigy of King Goorge VI. No coins bearing the date 1948 and 1949 were 
minted and issued by the Indian Mints . Pure Nickel Rupees , 1/2 Rupees and 1/4 Rupees 
of 1950 and on -Wurds bear on their obverse the Asoka Pillar and on the 
the Ears of coin design . In the Pure Nickel series of coins the rupees are security 
edged , whilst the 1/2 and 1/4 rupees bear & plain milling . 

Adoption of Pure Nickel in itself is a Bafegiiard ayainst counterfeiting . One 
of the Chief advantage lies in the difficulties of production Pure Nickel cannot 
reaclily be used for the making of small sand castings having a good surface 
finish . As the chief method of the Counterfeiter is to cast individual coins 
in sand or metal moulds, he must employ some alternative medium whiph con 
readily be detected . A distinctive feature of nickel is that it is magpetic, and 
no other metal which might conceivably be considered suitable for coinage has 
this property . Therefore a magnet can be used as a test of the genujueness of 
puro 

nickel coing. Further more piire nickel is particularly free from taruishing , 
any dirt or finger marks can be wiped clean with a dry rag. 

Two kinds of counterfeits of pure nickel coins are found viz . cast and struck , 
or & combination of the two . 

Cast counterfeits are the most easily made , and therefore are more usually 
found . The mould into which the molten metal is poured , may be made of 
sand , clay , powdered burnt brick , plaster of paris or similar materials and the 
impression on the mould is taken from a genuine coin . The mould has a small 
hole or gate out in the rim , through which the molten metal is poured . The 
metal which fills this hole , and solidifies with the rest of the casting, forms a 
projection on the rim of the cast coin , which must be cut off to enable the 
rim at this point to bo finished and milled by hand , and the security groove put in . 
Signs of this hand finishing can usually be detected on the rim of cast counterfeits . 


MANIPUR GAZETTE , OCTOBER 29 , 1955 . 


3 


ate 


of 


OT 


ar 


Struck counterfeito made by striking or 

preezing blanks batween dies of 
hard metal, the designs of which are engraved by hand , or from dies of softer 
metal on which the impressions can be transferred from t genuipe coin . The blanks , 
thus struck , may hrve been first cast or cut from sheet metal. 
Counterfeiti ara more easily detected if compared with one or 

more genuine 
coins of the same design , using a magnifying glass if necessary . A gopioivus cuin 
muy also be weighed against a genuine coin showing the same arount Wear , 
sinco most counterfeits which do not contain much lead are lighter than genuine 
coins . 

The only disadvantage of pure nickel coins is that they have no ring , and 
for centuries Indians have been accus omed to judge the genuineness of the silver 
coins by listening for the clear ring of high touch silver coins. There is no doubt 
that the ringing test diminished in importance when quaternary alloy coins with 
50 % silver were introduced . These coins yive a much big her and sharper ring than 
their high touch predecessor-. The cumbness of pure nickel coins however is com 
pressed for by their magnetic properties and any small boy equipped with a cheap 
magnet can easily distinguish a counterfeit from it genuine coin . 
The thickness of 

it genuine coin is uniforin whilst counterfeits are sometimes 
thicker at one side than at the other, and are often slightly bent distorted . 
Bu that they will not lie ovenly between two other coins. 

The rim of genuina pure nickel Rupees , 1/2 Rupers and 1/4 Rupees 
regularly miled and all round with straight indentations at right angles to the faces , 
but puic nickel rupees have also a security devise in the centre of tho willed udge . 
This device consists of a shallow groove with a design consisting of H 

chain of 
small beads in relief in two sections separated by blank spaces. In counterfeits of 
these coins the willing is often at a slant, the spaces between the indentations 
irregular and the indentations themselves uneven or broken . 

or broken . The milling can bent 
be examined by placing the suspected coin between two good coins of 

the some 
description , when defects can readily be detected . 
Coins should not be class :: d counterfeits solely op 

account of 

had mil 
ling, rince genuine coins are found with worn milling , with portions soldered for 
uge as ornaments or with the edges filled or touched up . Coins of which the milling 
is good however , are seldom counterfeits. 

The heads which appear in relief , inside the security groove on the Rupee 
coins, should be clear cut, well defined , and uniform in size and shape . The blank 
portions of the groove should be equal in length . In counterfeits, the beads are often 
badly shaped and uneven in size , the intervening spaces being sometimes filled with 
metal. The blank portions usually shown signs of metal baving been scooped out 
by hand , resulting the broken willing on either side of the blank portion , and 
possibly a wider groove in genuine coins. 
The devions on obver34 

glioull be clear and well definei, 
especially in outline . 

Blurred lines or edges and an imperfect impression (uoless 
plainly due to wear and ter] ar. suspicious. Letters and figures of the inscrip 
tion should be clear well defined and rounded . Blurred , irregular , or double lines 
are to be regarded with suspicion . In soile counterfeits the letter are much thinner 
than on genuine coins . 
The table or plain surface of the 

coin ( i. e. the portions not occupied by 
any design or ii scription ) should be smoth , oven and free from blemish . An upeyen , 
spotted or rough surface with irre zullar modes of metal thereon is siispicious.

th 


ail 


reverse 


MANIPUR GAZETTE , OCTOBER 29 , 1956 . 


All caut coins are counterfeits. In the ca - t coin the surface is usually rough , or 
pitted with minute holes . The rim is usually defective , particularly at the gate 
or point where the metal was poured int , the mould . 

In the case of struck counterfeits , it is not unusual to find serveral with indontical 
marks or defects, having bgen struck from the same dies . The milling is usually very 
irregular. In the case of cast counterfeits , the milling and the design in the groovo 
are often evenly spaced, except at the gate where the surplu , metal has been 
removed . 

Genuine coins which hive suffered by the action of chemicals of fire may bave rough 
and discolou, ed surfices though they are su -picious coins should be sent to one of the 
Mints for examination . 

Count: rfeits made from a mixture of tin and lead ar soft and easily bent, and 
will sometimes emit a crackling 10.8 ) when bent between the finge: 8 while being held 
close to the ear. 

The above hints are also help ul in dete :ting counterfeits of Cupro Nickel Coins 
and Bronze Coins of the years 1946 and onwards, exc pt that these coins are non 
magnetic and their designs were altered in the yeir 1950 . 

The two anna , one anna and half anna cupro -nickel coins of 1950) and onwards 
bear the Aroka Pillar on the obverse and the A.30x & Bull on the reverse . Their weights , 
sizes and shapes remaining the same as that of their prédecessor s . The Bronze single pice 
of 1950 bears the Asok . Pillar on the obserse and the Asok Horse in the reversh . It is 
circular in shape and weighs of 60 grains. From 9,1 onwards the bronze single pice 
Weighs 45 grains, all other features remaining the same as for 195 ) Agroept that the coins 
of 1951 and onwards are thinner than those of 1150), due to the reduction in wi: ht .

Kinds of coin and Legal Tender . 
20. Under the Indian Coinage Act (Act IJI of 1906 ) as amended from time to 
time the following coins have been issued : 

(i) Silver Rupee, half -rupee , quarter-rupee and eighth -rupee . The s andard weight 
of the rupeo is 180 grains troy , eleven -twelfth fine and the oth r silver ( oiries are 
of proportionate weight . Silver rup « es , half - rupees and quarter- rupous issued under

PART III 


INSPECTIONS . 


pre 


12. (1) Before granting or renewing an annual licence the licensing authority shall 

(a ) call upon the Executive Engineer to examine the structural features of the buil 

ding and report whether the rules relating thereto have been duly complied with ; 
(b ) call upon the Electric Iospector to examine the ciuematograph and the electrical 

equipment to be used in the building and to report , whether they comply with the 
requirements both of these rules and of the India Electricity Aot , 1910 , and of 
buch of the rules made thereunder fors are applicable, whether all reasonable 
cautions have been taken to protect spectators and employeos from electric shock 
and to prevent the introduction of fire into the building through the use of the 
electrical equipment, and whother the preboribed fire extinguishing appliances have 
buen provid d , are in working order anl are suitable for the purpose for which 

they are intended . 
(2 ) All defects revealed ly such inspections shall be brought to the notioe of the applicant 

or licensee and of the licensing authority , who may refuse to grant or renow the licence 

unless and until they are romediou on bis satisfaction . 
13. Before granting a temporary licenoe , and sub- oquently at such times as he may consider 

nedosrary , the licensing authority shall call upon the Executive Engineer to inspect the 
building anil to l aport whether it is structurally safe for use for cinematograph exhibitions . 
No foo shall be payable for such inspections, but before making his report the Executive 
Engineor may require the applicant for a licence or the license, as the case may be, to produce 
such drawings of the building as he may 

consider necessary and 

in ay at the 

request of 
the applicant or licensee cause all or any of the drawing to be prepared in his office , a 
reasonable fee for the work done in this respect being charged unler the special orders of the 
Chief Engineer, Central Public Words Departments, in each case , Defects revealed by any 
inspection shall be brought to the notice of the licensing authority, who may refuse to grant 
a licence or may revoke a licence already granted unless and until such defects are remedieda 

to his satisfaction . 
14. Before granting a temporary licence the licensing authority shall call upon the Electric Inspector 

to examine the electric appliances to be used in the cinema premises and to report as to their effi 
ciency and enfcty for purposes of oinematograph oxbibitions. Defects revealed by such inspections 
shall be brought to the notice of the licensing authority , who may refuse to grant a licence 

unless and until they are remedied to his satisfaction , 
15. The licensing authority or any officer authorised by him in this behalf may at any time 

enter a place which he has reason to believe is being used for the purpose of cinematograph 

exhibitions in order to satisfy himself that the conditions of the licence are being complied with . 
16. (1) The Executive Engineer , Electric Inspector or any ofioer specially appointed by the 

Executive Engineer or the Electriu Inspector in this behalf may at any time eater 

and inspect any place licensed under Section 10 of the Act . 
(2 ) A report of every euob inspection ball be made within forty -eight hours to tbe

the spread of the disease .

NOTIFICATION. 

Dated Shillong , the lat October, 1955 . 
No. TM / 10 /55 /31.-- It is notified for inforiit on and guidance of all Treasury 
Officers and Sub - Treasury Officers in Assam , Tripura and Manipur that the remitters 
applications relative to drawings effected by the Treasuries and Sub - Treasuries do not in 
most cases contain the serial numlier and the actual date of issue of drafts . The absence 
of these essential part culars in the applications makes their identification with the relative 
paid drafts very difficult on the part of the Reserve Bank of India, Central Accounts 
Section , Calcutta . Consequently it gives rise to avoidable correspondence with the Drawing 
Officers . It is therefore , requested that all necessary particulars should invariably be 
furnished in the applications in the spice provided for in the relative forms to avoid 
the difficulties so long experienced by the Reserve Bank of India . 

S. S. Lal, 
Deputy Accountant Gevorl (I ) , A - ram . 


FOR THE GAUHATI UNIVERSITY MATRICULATION 

EXAMI ATION , 1955 . 


Imphal, the 30th September, 1955 . 
Rules for the admission of private candidates to the preliminary Test Esamine 
tion tu be held under orders of the Inspector of School , Manipur . 

Under the regulations of the University the following rules on the above subject 
are published for general information : -- 
1. (a ) A candidate who has not attended any School for at least ono year pre 

vious to the examination , will he treated as a private candidate . 
(b ) Candidates who discontinued their studies while reading in the School 

will have to wait till the time, whou , if they continued their studies in a 
recognised School , they would have completed the Matriculation Course. 


2 


MANIPUR GAZETTE , OCTOBER 19 , 1955. 


(c ) Private candidates , from outside the territorial jurisdiction of the Gau 

hati University cannot be allowed to appear at the Test Examination unless 
they can produce the orders of the Gauhati University sanotioning their 

migrativn . 
( d ) No candidate frism & School which has not obtained formal recognition 

from the University will be recoynised as a private candidate even if he 
has read in a properly enrolled class of the School or has been taught 48 

a private pupil by one or more members of the staff , with or without fees. 
(e ) If any candidate in (d ) be eligible to appear as a private candidate under 

(b ) , he will be required to produce a certificate from the Headiuustor of 
the recognised School where be last read mentioning clearly the year and 

the class from which he discontinued there . 
2. Ordinarily touchers of recognised High and Middle English Schools and 
Librarians of recognised institution who intend to appear at the Matriculation Exa 
mination as privato candidate will not be required to appear at any Preliminary 
Test Examination if they have 32 months of continuous service to their credit . 
They should formally apply to the Registrar, Gauhati University , for permissi in to 
appear at the examination on or before 31st October, 1955 and should at the same 
timo comply with the following requirements. 

Each applicatiou should be addressed to the Registrar and should be submitted 
through the responsible person together with a certificate of yoot conduct and docu 
mentary evidence regarding age , residence and study and should contain a statement 
of the subjects proposed to be offered at the Examination . A te cher candidate should 
eubmit his application through the Head of the Institution in which he is einployed , 
countersigued by the Inspector of Schools. 

3. A detenu candidate also will be required to appear at the Preliminary Test 
Examination with permission from the Registrar. 

(a ) A detenu candidate , taking up Hygiene as an additional subject is to 

submit to the Registrar along with his application à certificate of training 
in the subject from a registered Medical Practitioner or from a recognised 

teacher of Hygiene . 
(b ) Teachers of recognised High or Middle English Schools taking up Hygiene 

will not be required to submit any such certificate. 
4. Applications of teacher or detenu candidates must be accompanied by a por 
mission fee of Rs . 18 /-. No application will be considered without permission fee . The 
University Treasurer s Chalan or Postal Money Order receipt in support of the pay 
ment of permission fee must be attached in the application . 

5 . All private candidates must pass the Test Examination held in any of the 
following High Schools ; 

1. Johnstone High School, Imphal. 

Ukhrul High School, Ukhrul.

eases and appeals which may bi ready for h aring during ihe long vacıtion will be disposed 
of by the District & Sessions Juage , Manipur . During the va ation he will sit on the 
17th October , 1955 and fun the 28th Octolier 1955 10 31st October , 1955 and from 
the 3rd November, 1955 to the 9th November , 1975 during the long vacat.on and on 
the 24th and 25th December, 1955 during the Christmas hol days as vacation Judge. 


The office of the court will be opened from 10 a.m. to 4 p.m. during the periods as 
mentioned above and the remaining period of the long vacation and christinas holidays, 
the office will be opened from 12 noon to 4 p.m. every day except on saturdays and
